#2611. Aaron Rodgers benched vs. Broncos; Steelers will start Mason Rudolph at QB instead

The Pittsburgh Steelers announced Tuesday night that Mason Rudolph will start at quarterback on Friday against the Denver Broncos. Rodgers will be inactive.

NFL insider Tom Pelissero reported after the announcement that the decision was head coach Mike McCarthy's. There are no reports that Rodgers is dealing with an injury.

The Steelers didn't offer any explanation beyond a statement from team spokesman Burt Lauten announcing the roster move:

"The Pittsburgh Steelers have named Mason Rudolph the starting quarterback for the game this Friday against the Denver Broncos.

"Aaron Rodgers will be inactive for the game on Friday."

Rodgers, a future Hall of Famer, may have played his last NFL game. He announced before the season started that this would be his last in the league. It's a stunning development for the 6-4 Steelers and a quarterback who is one of the best to ever play the game.

Aaron Rodgers is no longer the starting quarterback for the Pittsburgh Steelers.

Rodgers, 42, is in his 22nd NFL season and well beyond his prime. He's in his second season with the Steelers, who hired McCarthy in the offseason in part because of his previous relationship with Rodgers.

McCarthy coached Rodgers for 13 seasons during Rodgers' prime and the pair won a Super Bowl together in Green Bay. Rodgers won two his four NFL MVP's while playing for McCarthy.

But with a playoff berth in the balance, McCarthy and the Steelers have decided 10 games into their season that Rudolph gives them a better chance at making the postseason.

Tuesday's news drops one day after McCarthy gave Rodgers what appeared to be clear vote of confidence.

"Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback," McCarthy told reporters in his Monday news conference.

That statement came on the heels of Sunday's 19-16 loss to the Philadelphia Eagles. The loss was the second straight for Pittsburgh and snapped a seven-game losing streak for an Eagles team that's been in a tailspin for most of the season.

Even with the consecutive losses, the Steelers remain on the right side of the playoff picture. If the postseason started this week, Pittsburgh would play as the final wild-card team and the No. 7 seed in the AFC.

Rodgers had displayed continued signs of decline this year with statistically his worst career season. But there are worse starting quarterbacks in the NFL. And it's questionable whether Rudolph, an established NFL backup, gives the Steelers a better chance at winning.

In 10 starts, Rodgers has completed 60.9% of his passes for 6.2 yards per attempt and 224.1 yards per game with 16 touchdowns and 6 interceptions. The Steelers rank 23rd in total offense, 22nd in passing offense and 22nd in scoring offense with 21.4 points per game.

Rudolph is a seven-year NFL veteran in his sixth season with a Steelers team that selected him in the third round of the 2018 NFL Draft.

In 38 career appearances, Rudolph has completed 64.2% of his passes for 6.6 yards per attempt with 30 touchdown and 22 interceptions. He's made 19 career starts, but has never established himself as an NFL starter.

With their postseason hopes on the line against a stout Broncos defense, the Steelers are ready to give Rudolph another chance in favor of their aging future Hall of Famer.

#2610. Nets searching for answers as early defensive collapses fuel losing streak

MINNEAPOLIS — The Nets will drag the NBA's longest losing skid into Milwaukee on Wednesday, a five-game slump, and pretty much all five losses have looked the same.

Embarrass themselves defensively in the first half. Give themselves no chance to win in the second. Scratch their heads looking for answers in the postgame.

Head coach Jordi Fernandez argues a call during the Nets' loss to the Magic in an NBA Cup game on Nov. 20, 2026 at Barclays Center. Getty Images

"That's happened already too many times, where we allow teams [to get on a roll early]. It's the best players in the world. If you allow one to go in and then everybody gets loose and makes shots, then it's a long night," Jordi Fernández said.

When asked what the biggest problem with their defense was, Julius Randle couldn't even pinpoint one.

"We've got a lot. We were better earlier in the year and now we've got slippage in multiple areas. So we've gotta figure out where it is. But defense just starts with competitive nature first and then go from there," Randle said. "It's disappointing. But all you can do is focus on where you're at and figure out how to get better. Figure out what's the problem, how we can consistently compete at a high level."

The problem is, in this skid the Nets have barely competed at any level.

CHECK OUT THE LATEST NBA STANDINGS AND NETS STATS

They've allowed more than 70 points in the first half of four of the five losses, tied with Charlotte for the league's longest slump. They've shown a concerning glass jaw, getting hit early and not hitting back.

"Defensively, we just can't give up 140 points. We let [Minnesota] catch their rhythm early, and that's what happens when you give good teams rhythm early on in the game," Mikel Brown Jr. told The Post. "We've got to cut that water off early, take away those wide-open shots."

In this five-game malaise, the Nets have allowed an average of 72.2 points and a 147.3 defensive rating in their first halves. They've conceded 55.9 percent shooting and 52.3 percent from deep and a minus-15.0 net rating.

Every single one of those stats is not only dead last in the league, but the worst by a margin.

That kind of porous defense in the first half makes the second half all but moot.

"Yes, it's always been for us to get off to a good start," Brown told The Post. "I really just feel like just disrupting them early on. Anytime we disrupt the team early on in the game, we come out and we fight hard. And sometimes we pull out the win, and sometimes we don't.

"But it's really just the early [part] in the game. You just can't let good players get great shots early on in the games. You give those looks to those great players, they're gonna start flowing, and then next thing you know, they shoot 50 percent, 55 percent from the field as a team. It's a snowball effect that we just can't have."

#2605. NFL news, live updates: Steelers bench Aaron Rodgers, will start Mason Rudolph in Week 12; Brandon Aiyuk to visit Commanders as a free agent on Wednesday

Week 12 of the NFL season kicks off with a rare Wednesday matchup between the Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams, prepping us for a full Thanksgiving feast of football the following day, headlined by an MVP showcase between the Buffalo Bills and Kansas City Chiefs on Thursday night.

Ahead of that matchup, the Steelers made headlines by reportedly benching veteran quarterback and former MVP Aaron Rodgers in favor of backup Mason Rudolph for Week 12 after recent struggles on offense. Rodgers will be inactive for Friday's game against the Denver Broncos, per Steelers spokesman Burt Lauten.

We're watching plenty of injuries ahead of the action, including Chiefs running back Kenneth Walker III, who's missed the last two games with a groin injury suffered in practice. He was officially limited on Tuesday. Fantasy football managers will also be keeping a Las Vegas Raiders All-Pro tight end Brock Bowers, who missed Week 11 with an ankle injury, though he was reportedly pushing to play in that matchup.

The week also saw two players hit free agency after clearing waivers, including former Indianapolis Colts quarterback Daniel Jones and former San Francisco 49ers wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk. Aiyuk reportedly has a visit scheduled with the Washington Commanders on Wednesday, who are short on receiver help after a season-ending knee injury to Terry McLaurin, while Jones' future remains up in the air.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for the latest NFL news, injury updates and more coming out of Week 11.

The Pittsburgh Steelers announced Tuesday night that Mason Rudolph will start at quarterback on Friday against the Denver Broncos.

The Eagles listed star running back Saquon Barkley as a full practice participant on Tuesday.

The injury report did note that it was just a walkthrough, but yesterday, when Philly did not practice, the team listed Barkley as a limited practice participant due to a neck injury as well as an illness.

The Eagles play the Cowboys on Thursday afternoon for Thanksgiving.

Brandon Aiyuk may have a new home sooner than later.

The former 49ers receiver is scheduled to visit with the Washington Commanders. That's according to his agent, Drew Rosenhaus, who told ESPN's Adam Schefter that Aiyuk will meet with the Commanders on Wednesday.

Ayiuk cleared waivers on Tuesday after previously being released by the 49ers, concluding a protracted and contentious breakup with the franchise.

After finally being released by the San Francisco 49ers, veteran wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk has cleared waivers, according to ESPN's Adam Schefter, who also reported Tuesday that Aiyuk would like to sign with a new team soon.

He's expected to attract interest from a few teams, including the Washington Commanders, per Schefter.

The Niners releasing Aiyuk marked the end of a months-long standoff between the team and player. Aiyuk missed the entire 2025 NFL season after recovering from a knee injury he suffered in 2024, just months after signing a four-year, $120 million extension with the team. He suffered a torn ACL and MCL in late October 2024 and hasn't played a snap in the league since.

San Francisco had a two-week roster exemption for Aiyuk. It expired on Monday, and as a result, the franchise opted to release Aiyuk, giving both the team and the wide receiver a fresh start.

Denver Broncos running back J.K. Dobbins has been suspended for one game for conduct detrimental to the team, according to head coach Sean Payton.

Payton did not get into specifics and told reporters he would not comment further on the situation.

Dobbins, 27, is in his second season with the Broncos. He has rushed for 324 yards and 3 touchdowns on 99 carries, adding 31 receiving yards on 9 catches. Last year, Dobbins compiled 772 rushing yards and 4 TDs.

The suspension leaves Denver will be without its leading rusher for Friday's Week 12 matchup with the Pittsburgh Steelers. Jonah Coleman (302 rushing yards in 6 games) will presumably take over as the starter.

Micah Parsons is officially activated off the Physically Unable to Perform list, Green Bay Packers general manager Brian Gutekunst announced on Tuesday.

So the All-Pro pass rusher appears to be ready to play in Wednesday's Thanksgiving Eve matchup versus the Los Angeles Rams. Parsons had been listed as questionable for the game.

Parsons has been sidelined while recovering from a torn ACL and meniscus suffered during Week 15 last season. Prior to his injury, he compiled 12.5 sacks in 14 games (13 starts) in his first year with Green Bay.

While Green Bay Packers edge rusher Micah Parsons is reportedly poised to make his long-awaited return from his ACL injury Wednesday night against the Los Angeles Rams, he's officially listed as "questionable" for the Week 12 matchup.

In Parsons' first, abridged season with the Packers, he piled up 12.5 sacks over 14 games.

Running back Josh Jacobs is also "questionable" for the Thanksgiving Eve game at SoFi Stadium.

He's been recovering from surgery on the ring finger he fractured.

The Kansas City Chiefs have been cautious handling the groin injury that running back Kenneth Walker III is navigating.

The Chiefs will travel to Buffalo this week for a Thanksgiving showdown with the Bills.

If there's anything that can slow this 49ers team, it's the blue medical tent. Injuries have ravaged the 49ers all season, and on Sunday night, San Francisco ran out a Geriatric Avengers of thirty-somethings including George Kittle, Mike Evans, Christian McCaffrey, Deebo Samuel Sr. and Kyle Juszczyk. All that experience gives the 49ers a big-game-pressure edge that few teams can match, but it also makes them intensely vulnerable to injury. Stints on the IR are a when, not an if, for the 49ers. Right tackle Colton McKivitz was the latest as he suffered a gruesome leg injury in the first half Sunday night.

The guy ensuring all those uncs get the ball is finally receiving some attention of his own again, too. Brock Purdy hasn't shown up on MVP leaderboards since finishing fourth in his breakout season of 2023. This season ought to change that. He's among the league leaders in touchdowns, passer rating and completion percentage, among other useful stats.

Against the best defense in the NFL, all Purdy did was throw for three touchdowns and 255 yards on 21-of-26 completions. He's grown into the perfect weapon for head coach Kyle Shanahan's unorthodox offense, and Sunday was the perfect showcase.

McCarthy was asked on Monday whether he was considering a quarterback change after the offense's poor play in Sunday's 19-16 loss to the Philadelphia Eagles.

McCarthy kept it short and to the point, saying, "Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback."

Pittsburgh dropped to 6-4 after the loss and fell to third place in the AFC North, trailing both the Cincinnati Bengals and the Cleveland Browns.

The Steelers had 255 yards of offense but couldn't beat the Eagles. Philadelphia's win ended a seven-game losing streak for the franchise, and the Steelers have now lost two straight games, both coming after their Week 9 bye.

In the loss, Rodgers was 30-of-44 passing for 227 yards and a touchdown. He led the team in rushing with 29 yards on five carries, but was sacked five times.

If the New England Patriots are interested in adding quarterback Daniel Jones, head coach Mike Vrabel isn't saying so.

Taking over after Drake Maye was injured in Week 10's game versus the Detroit Lions, DeVito completed 12-of-24 passes for 81 yards with 1 touchdown and 1 interception. Vrabel said he was impressed by the backup QB's performance and the improvement he's shown since training camp, even if the Patriots didn't win the game.

The plan is for Sam Darnold to start against the 49ers on Sunday, but Mike Macdonald wants to see the quarterback in practice first before making a final decision.

Darnold has been out for two games due to a quad injury suffered in Week 8.

Before Week 11, the New York Giants had a perception problem.

They hadn't beaten a team with a winning record. Their advanced metrics said they were a below-average team. Their point differential was +7, which was meager for a 5-3-1 team. They looked like a team lucky to be playing in the putrid NFC East.

Then on Sunday, the Giants got a win that can help legitimize their start. They beat a Jaguars team that was a major buyer at the trade deadline and looked like one of the NFL's elite teams. It was easily the Giants' biggest win this season, and for a team that has missed the playoffs eight of the past nine seasons, one of their biggest victories in many years. It might change what is being said about the Giants.

"We don't really worry about that, we don't really care what they think or what they say," Giants head coach John Harbaugh said after the game, via the team's transcripts. "It's not our concern how we're evaluated. It's our concern how we play."

Yet, they knew what was being said about them.

After missing nearly a year with tears to his ACL and meniscus, Micah Parsons is set to return to the Packers' defensive line this week. The star linebacker has reportedly been cleared to play as of Monday, per NFL Network's Ian Rapoport.

Parsons reportedly needed one final clearance on his knee before getting the green light, and will now make his debut against the Rams on Wednesday.

New York Giants cornerback Greg Newsome will be sidelined for "multiple weeks" with a high ankle sprain, head coach John Harbaugh confirmed on Monday.

It's still unclear if Newsome will need surgery or if he will land on injured reserve, which would knock him out for at least four games.

"It's a serious high ankle. It's gonna be one of those lengthy high ankles," Harbaugh said, via ESPN. "It's going to be multiple weeks."

For more on Newsome's injury, click here.

San Francisco is finally parting ways with wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk.

After a months-long saga around the receiver wanting to be released, Aiyuk was officially waived by the 49ers on Monday. That confirms reports earlier on Monday indicating that Aiyuk would finally be released.

Other teams will have until Wednesday to claim Aiyuk, who will become a free agent after he clears waivers.

Former Colts quarterback Daniel Jones was not claimed on Monday, thus making him a free agent.

Jones is free to sign with any team that wants him, and that team can pay him the veteran minimum.

The Colts still owe Jones about $20 million.

The Commanders have activated running back Jacory Croskey-Merritt from injured reserve, and he is off the injury report.

JCM will play Monday night against the Bengals. He hasn't played since Week 5 due to a groin injury.

So far this season, Croskey-Merritt has 239 yards and two touchdowns on 67 carries. He also has 40 yards on five receptions.

The Cardinals have reportedly fired defensive coordinator Nick Rallis, per NFL Network's Ian Rapoport.

Arizona's defense has allowed 355.2 yards per game this season, the seventh-most in the league. The Cardinals' allowing 29.4 points per game is the second-most in the league, trailing only the Raiders at 29.7 points per game.

San Francisco 49ers general manager John Lynch has agreed to a multi-year contract extension with the team, according to reports.

Lynch and team owner Jed York agreed to the new contract with a handshake while on the sideline at Estadio Banorte before Sunday night's 35-10 win over the Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City, Fox Sports' Jay Glazer reported.

Exact terms of Lynch's extension have not yet been reported. The 49ers have not officially announced the agreement. Lynch's current contract was set to expire after this season.

Lynch's new deal joins an extension for head coach Kyle Shanahan, who reached agreement on the contract in September shortly after the 49ers played the Los Angeles Rams in Melbourne, Australia. San Francisco's international games this season have been extremely lucrative for the team's top football authority figures.

#2604. Tuesday's CIAC football playoff update: Newtown among four to unofficially qualify for CIAC playoffs, 14 berths remain

Four more teams unofficially clinched CIAC football playoff berths Tuesday night, according to GametimeCT projections, bringing the statewide total to 34 as Thanksgiving Week's football schedule got underway.

That leaves 14 playoff berths up for grabs heading into Wednesday's games. Our projections are determined using the CIAC playoff formula, using OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna medium model and verified by GametimeCT using the current CIAC football playoff standings.

The points are not official until the CIAC football playoff committee meets and approves the final tally the day after all the games are completed on Thanksgiving Day.

In, CLASS LL, FAIRFIELD PREP (6-4) clinched with its 38-15 victory over Xavier, narrowing the available spots to three, mainly between NFA, Hamden, and South Windsor, though Glastonbury and the winner of Stamford-Westhill remained mathematically alive.

LEWIS MILLS/WOLCOTT TECH (8-2) earned its first state playoff berth in school history with its 17-0 victory over Avon/St. Paul, leaving three spots available.

In CLASS MM, NEWTOWN (7-2) clinched when Xavier lost to Fairfield Prep, leaving three spots available. Despite the loss, XAVIER (6-4) remains alive but now needs help - namely two of Platt, Torrington or Wilton to lose, or it'll be in trouble.

TORRINGTON (6-3) and PLATT (6-3) can now clinch with wins on Thanksgiving. WILTON (5-4) needs to win and get help. NEW MILFORD (6-4) remained mathematically alive with its 34-6 Candlewood Cup victory over New Fairfield, but is a longshot to steal a spot.

THAMES RIVER CO-OP (8-2) failed to clinch a home playoff game with its 20-7 loss to Montville/St. Bernard and will now await other results to improve its positioning.

VALLEY REGIONAL/OLD LYME (7-3) clinched by virtue of its 47-28 victory over Haddam-Killingworth in the Principal's Cup, simultaneously eliminating Haddam-Killingworth.

Also, TOLLAND's 29-7 victory over E.O. Smith clinched the Eagles a home quarterfinal. MONTVILLE/ST. BERNARD (6-4) remained alive with its 20-7 victory over Thames River co-op.

There are 17 games scheduled for Wednesday, including several moved up to avoid rainy weather forecasts on Thanksgiving morning.

Among some of the big games with playoff implications:

Woodland (6-3) at Seymour (6-3), 6 p.m. - The winner clinches in Class S, while the loser will be relying on outside help to clinch.

Holy Cross (8-1) at Wolcott (6-3), 6 p.m.

- Wolcott must beat Holy Cross to stay alive in the Class S race. Losing will help the other contenders. Holy Cross is looking to improve its seeding in Class MM.

Coginchaug co-op (6-3) at North Branford (9-0), 6 p.m. - Coginchaug must beat the unbeaten T-Birds to clinch a spot in Class S, or hope outside results go its way. North Branford is already the No. 1 seed in the class.

Fairfield Warde (6-3) at Fairfield Ludlowe (9-0), 6 p.m. - Warde has an outside shot at a Class L berth, but it must beat unbeaten Ludlowe, which is vying for a top seed in the class.

Rockville (7-2) at Ellington (5-4), 6:30 p.m. - Ellington must win to keep its playoff hopes alive in Class M heading into Thanksgiving morning. Then it will need Waterford to lose to 1-8 East Lyme. Rockville's hoping to get a quarterfinal home game with a win coupled with a Guilford loss.

Guilford (7-2) at Hand (9-0), 7 p.m. - Guilford can clinch a home game in Class M, but it hasn't beaten the Tigers since 2006. Hand is already the No. 1 seed in class SS.

South Windsor (4-5) at Enfield (2-7), 6 p.m. - The Bobcats must win to get in line for a Class LL playoff spot. It still needs help, specifically a Hamden loss on Thanksgiving.

Farmington (6-3) at Plainville (9-0), 6 p.m. - Farmington needs to beat unbeaten Plainville to stay alive in Class L while Plainville is hoping to clinch the No. 2 seed in SS with its first unbeaten season in 80 years.

Xavier's loss to Fairfield Prep relieved Newtown of playoff pressure heading into this game. Both teams hope to improve their seeding. For Newtown, a home playoff game. For Masuk, potentially the No. 1 seed in the Class M playoffs.

#2602. Brandon Williams on Warriors offense without Steph Curry: ‘That s*** was tough’

Watching the Golden State Warriors sputter offensively from the sidelines has tested Brandon Williams' patience, and the dynamic guard is itching to inject some much-needed life back into the rotation. Officially listed as questionable for Wednesday's matchup against the Sacramento Kings, Williams made it clear to reporters that he feels ready to suit up and make an immediate impact.

Asked about the frustration of watching Golden State's offense stall while sidelined, Williams did not mince words. "That shit was tough, I ain't gonna lie," he admitted. "I know exactly what I need to do."

That blunt self-assurance is a welcome sign for a Warriors squad desperate for perimeter punch and pace. If cleared before tip-off against Sacramento, Williams' ability to attack the paint, collapse defenses, and create quick-hitting scoring opportunities could be the exact spark Golden State needs to snap out of its offensive funk.

In the five games and around 90 percent of the game that Curry has missed (the 90 percent being the game against the Houston Rockets in which he was injured), the Warriors have scored a league-worst 98 points per 100 possessions in non-garbage time, per Cleaning the Glass — a far cry from the 118.9 points per 100 possessions they were scoring in their first 11 games, where they had an 8-3 record and were exceeding expectations.

Williams is no Curry, but he does provide stability as a ball handler and will drive the Warriors' pick-and-roll offense as a speedy guard who draws fouls.

#2599. New manager, fresh hope. But why the stats say Al Faisaly don't need overhaul

Mohamed Dahman replaces Giovanni Solinas in the dugout ahead of his debut match against fellow new boys Diriyah Club

It might seem a rather obvious statement, but football is all about goals.

The important distinction, however, is it is just as important to deny them as it is to score them. Just look at promoted sides Al Faisaly and Diriyah Club.

Both started the 2026-27 Roshn Saudi League with different expectations and ambitions. Yet despite Diriyah Club signing Darwin Nunez, it is actually Al Faisaly that has scored the more goals this season out of the two - 13 goals to 10.

The issue is, though, that Al Faisaly have conceded almost twice as many (24 to 13) and that is the story of their respective campaigns so far.

Mohamed Dahman takes temporary charge of Al Faisaly following Giovanni Solinas' resignation

There is much to like in how the Al Majma'ah-based side have played and, although manager Giovanni Solinas recently resigned, his interim replacement, Mohamed Dahman, inherits a squad that is already halfway there.

At present, Al Faisaly are the league's No.1-ranked pressing team, applying high-pressure to 46.3 percent of opposition touches, yet their opponents complete 79.2% of passes under that pressure.

The former is proof they have the style right; they now just need to increase its effectiveness. That is, forcing more errors and turning the ball over more often. That's easier said than done, of course, but it's a better place to start than the alternative.

Giovanni Solinas leaves Al Faisaly with the club one spot above the relegation zone

The key focus for Dahman against Diriyah Club when the two RSL new boys meet in Matchweek 13 on Thursday must be on how the hosts start the game. That's because the statistics reveal that Al Faisaly have conceded a league-high number of goals (14) in the first half.

Drilling down even further, 13 of those have been conceded in the opening 30 minutes - also a league high - and eight between minutes 16 and 30. That latter? You guessed it, a league high.

Contrast that to Diriyah Club, who are yet to give away a goal in the opening 15 minutes, and have conceded only six in the first half, and the contrast hits home.

"We exploited the opposition's defensive confusion after the first goal," explained Al Fayha coach Fabio Carille late last month, after his team's Sudair Derby success against Al Faisaly.

So, for Al Faisaly, tightening their belts defensively must be priority No.1. That said, it's not through lack of effort: they sit second for tackles and third for interceptions in the RSL this season.

They're pressing high, they're winning tackles and intercepting the ball but, despite all of that, they still have the league's third-worst defensive record.

Yet it's not all bad news. There are plenty of positives that can give Al Faisaly cause for optimism heading into the Buraidah clash with Diriyah Club.

Chief among those is the threat provided by the crossing ability of Yassen Barnawi, who has recorded a league-high successful crosses from open play (13).

Al Faisaly take on an upwardly mobile Diriyah Club, lead by on-loan star Darwin Nunez

What makes that so threatening is the fact that five of Al Faisaly's 13 goals (38%) have been headers - again ranked No.1 across the league. With almost a third of Diriyah Club's goals conceded this term being headers, that offers a unique opportunity to exploit.

In fact, this is something opposition managers are already aware of, with Carille commenting before the Sudair Derby: "We watched [Al Faisaly's] previous match against Abha, and we know how dangerous their crosses are."

In that, they also have the perfect focal point to get on the end of it in Alexandre Mendy, who has five goals in the RSL and ranks sixth in the division for total shots (19).

Summer signing Alexandre Mendy has five goals in 12 RSL games for Al Faisaly

That backs up the fact Al Faisaly have more goals than Diriyah Club through the opening 12 rounds. Also, it illustrates that they are generating and scoring enough, relatively speaking, when they attack.

And, as noted, it only amplifies the need to tighten up defensively.

Do that and the four draws they've had could easily have been turned into wins. Still, with seven points in their past four games, there is enough to believe 15th-placed Al Faisaly aren't far away from flipping their fortunes.

Thursday, against a fellow promoted side albeit with different aspirations, represents a decent place to start.

#2594. Knicks’ fading NBA Cup hopes on line against Cavaliers

The Knicks' slim NBA Cup hopes hang in the balance.

Wednesday's game against the Cavs at Madison Square Garden is the Knicks' third of four group-stage games, having dropped their first two group-stage games to the 76ers and Pacers, respectively.

In the three-year history of the Cup, no team has ever advanced to the knockout round with anything worse than a 3-1 record. The winner of each of the six groups along with two wild cards (the top team from each conference that finished second in its group) advances to the next round.

Jalen Brunson (left) and Mikal Bridges high five during the Knicks' win over the Pacers on Nov. 22, 2026 at Madison Square Garden. NBAE via Getty Images

So while those first two losses didn't officially eliminate the Knicks, they put them in a deep hole to defend their NBA Cup title. The Knicks enter Wednesday's game last in their group and as the only one without a win.

Another loss Wednesday would officially eliminate the Knicks.

Wednesday's matchup is already the third meeting between the Knicks and Cavs this year, having split the first two. The Knicks lost the most recent matchup 115-107 on Nov. 11 at MSG.

"They were real physical in Game 2," coach Mike Brown said after practice Tuesday. "You give them credit. They talked about keeping us off the glass and some other things and they did it. They're a great coached team with Kenny Atkinson and a great team in general.

"There's gonna have to be a lot of things that we do better in this game, starting with the level of physicality, while getting out in transition, if we expect to get a win."

Anunoby's injury changed from calf soreness to a calf strain, but the Knicks are still optimistic that he and Clarkson should be short-term absences and consider the duo day to day.

Josh Hart (right shoulder sprain), Karl-Anthony Towns (right knee inflammation) and Miles McBride (left adductor tightness) are all questionable. All three practiced Tuesday, though.

#2591. Pep Guardiola admits he was 'embarrassed' to reject Italy job as former Man City boss confirms national team management is his 'next step'

Pep Guardiola has broken his silence on his decision to turn down the opportunity to manage Italy, admitting he felt "embarrassed" to say no to the Azzurri. The legendary tactician, currently on a sabbatical following a trophy-laden decade at Manchester City, also confirmed that his next move in football will be onto the international stage.

In July, after Italy missed a third consecutive World Cup, the Italian federation (FIGC) sent Paolo Maldini and Leonardo to approach Guardiola. The offer arrived shortly after he left Manchester City following a 2016–2026 stint yielding six Premier League titles, three FA Cups and a Champions League crown.

Drained by the grind, the former Barcelona and Bayern Munich manager chose a self-imposed sabbatical of at least one year. Italy subsequently re-hired Roberto Mancini, their Euro 2020-winning coach from 2018 to 2023.

Speaking on Romario TV on Tuesday with Brazil legend and former Barcelona team-mate Romario, Guardiola explained why timing scuppered the Italy move. He also confirmed his desire to eventually coach internationally while insisting he remains content away from the touchline.

"This is the first time I speak about this, but yes, I did talk to them," Guardiola said. "I felt embarrassed to say 'no' to Italy, a country like Italy, it would have been one of the greatest honours of my career to coach the Italian national team. But I realised I needed to stop. If they had called two years earlier, I probably would have accepted.

"But when Paolo Maldini and Leonardo, whom I love dearly, reached out, it was at a point where I simply had to stop. I needed some time for myself. After 18 years as a coach, with matches every three days, week in and week out, there comes a moment when you just have to say 'phew.'"

Identifying "a national team" as his sole unfulfilled coaching ambition - and "that is the next step" for his career - Guardiola stressed he is in no rush to return. "The truth is, I don't know, I really don't know. But for now, I'm doing fine," he added. "If I ever return, it will be because my gut tells me to, and because someone wants me, then I'll go back. But it's been many years, so many matches."

England, managed by Thomas Tuchel, and the Netherlands, who recently hired Xavi, have both been linked with Guardiola, alongside USMNT following Mauricio Pochettino's exit to Manchester United.

At his final press conference, Pochettino called it "the perfect moment to arrive" at the USMNT and urged Guardiola to take the job: "Everything is in the right direction. I think, of course, take [it]. Take it because [Guardiola is] going to find really good people, good players. The player can fit for his style; the National Training Center that wasn't [there] before."

Suitors must remain patient as Guardiola continues his minimum one-year break away from the dugout. Without a set timeline or destination, his eventual return to international management depends entirely on when his instincts tell him he is ready for a new challenge.

#2590. Steelers bench Aaron Rodgers in stunning move in QB's final season

In a stunning development late Tuesday, Nov. 24, the Pittsburgh Steelers announced that quarterback Mason Rudolph would start in Week 12's Black Friday game against the Denver Broncos. Team PR spokesman Burt Lauten gave no reason for the change but only revealed that Aaron Rodgers, the team's QB1 since the start of the 2025 season, would be inactive for a matchup with significant playoff ramifications for both teams.

However, according to The Ringer's Tom Pelissero, the pivot from Rodgers to Rudolph is a performance-based one that was made by head coach Mike McCarthy and effectively a benching of the 42-year-old four-time league MVP.

In Pittsburgh's most recent game, a 19-16 defeat to the largely decimated Philadelphia Eagles, who had lost seven games in a row entering last Sunday's contest, Rodgers completed 30 of 44 passes but only for 227 yards and a TD. Rodgers' 6.2 yards per pass attempt is the lowest of his 22-year career since he became a full-time starter in 2008 and discounting his 2023 campaign with the New York Jets, when he suffered a season-ending Achilles tear on the first drive of opening night.

The first-year coach and native Pittsburgher spoke to reporters hours before the team's late-night announcement about Rodgers. McCarthy gave no indication he would bench a player he's been paired with most of his head coaching career, the duo spending nearly 13 seasons together with the Green Bay Packers.

"I just think the first thing comes to mind is wealth of knowledge and experience. I mean, there's really not a spot that he hasn't been in, so I think that's clearly evident. Our conversation this morning, probably half of it was going through different games over the years that reminds you of this, reminds you of that, the team we're getting ready to play," McCarthy said earlier Tuesday when asked about Rodgers.

"So, he just has a tremendous amount of experience and expertise, and I think that's a tool that his teammates and coaches are fortunate to have."

McCarthy did say as recently as Monday that "Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback" when asked about the possibility of mixing some of the other teams passers into the gameplan.

The coach also lamented the team's recent lack of offensive balance on Tuesday and the resulting impact on the pass game.

"We need to run the ball better, but we need to run it more," said McCarthy. "I mean, it's just like anything − a good run game starts with attempts. So, but we definitely got to run the ball, and then with that, the action pass game opportunities I feel will increase."

After a midseason surge in production from running back Jaylen Warren and the ground game, the Steelers have only managed 175 rushing yards total while dropping their past two games. And despite being in the top quartile of passes thrown league-wide, Pittsburgh has not gotten many explosive plays through the air nor sufficient production from wideouts like DK Metcalf.

The coach and quarterback have mostly had a very successful NFL collaboration over the course of their relationship, notably teaming to take the Packers to victory in Super Bowl 45, that storied franchise's most recent championship, to cap the 2010 season.

However the relationship had frayed to a degree by the time McCarthy was fired in Green Bay with four games left in the 2018 season. Rodgers has largely plastered over that period in subsequent years and typically speaks highly of McCarthy.

However the quarterback was caught on Prime Video's cameras in a Week 4 loss at Cleveland while screaming at McCarthy from the field and demanding a play call. The coach was subsequently caught muttering an expletive with a frustrated look on his face.

"I think it is a positive," Rodgers said in the aftermath while downplaying the incident. "Player-led teams are the best teams. I think anytime you're seeing the fire and the passion from all those guys, it's a good thing."

McCarthy has not addressed Rodgers' deactivation for the upcoming game, and Lauten's statement provided no insight into the decision. No NFL rule would preclude the Steelers from reversing course or asking Rodgers to back up Rudolph. But a decision as seismic as this one, combined with Rodgers' stature, would seem to make that highly unlikely. The Steelers also did not specify if Rodgers could return as the starter in Week 13, but that also seems improbable, barring further information.

Now in his second year with the Steelers, Rodgers had already indicated the 2026 season would be his final one. His one-year contract is set to pay him $22.5 million, fully guaranteed.

A ninth-year veteran, Rudolph was selected by the Steelers in the third round of the 2018 draft. He's spent all but one season of his NFL career in Pittsburgh, mostly serving as a backup to Rodgers, Ben Roethlisberger and Kenny Pickett. Rudolph has been one of the league's better Plan B QBs, going 8-5-1 in 14 regular-season starts for Pittsburgh.

Yet this also isn't the first time the Steelers have called him out of the bullpen late in the season. Rudolph replaced then-QB2 Mitch Trubisky late in the 2023 season when Pickett was injured. However then-coach Mike Tomlin kept Rudolph in the lineup, including Pittsburgh's playoff game at Buffalo, even when Pickett was healthy enough to play. Rudolph ended the regular season by winning all three of his starts and compiling a gaudy 118.0 passer rating. However, the overmatched Steelers were soundly beaten 31-17 by the Bills in the wild-card round, though Rudolph passed for 229 yards and two TDs.

#2589. Jaguars-Giants boosts late Sunday window to more than 26 million

The 4:25 p.m. ET window on Sunday continues to produce the biggest numbers of the NFL week.

#2585. ‘Got to be…’ – Maxx Crosby sounds off on Bijan Robinson’s take about the easiest position in the NFL

Las Vegas Raiders DE Maxx Crosby did not appreciate Atlanta Falcons RB Bijan Robinson's take that the edge rusher is the easiest position in the NFL.

Every elite or contending team is looking to build their offence around a world-class QB and their defence around a world-class edge rusher. Defensive players usually play within a system, so the EDGE is the one defender who is designed to be an independent unit responsible for sacking a slow QB.

Unfortunately, the limited scope of the role led to Falcons RB Robinson dismissing the position as the easiest in the NFL, irking standout EDGE Crosby.

Crosby, a former All-Pro EDGE, responded to Robinson's thoughts on 'The Rush Podcast,' justifying their value while tearing down the position Robinson stars in.

"Casual a– take. Look at it like this: who is your highest-paid player (QB)? And who is next (EDGE)? If it were easy to play the position and get one sack a game, why doesn't everybody do it? Out of 1,000 snaps, getting 10 sacks is great. You're considered elite. Running back? You get handed the ball, and you go play football. You run, and you catch. Running back is, easily, got to be the easiest position to play."

It might be easier to be an average EDGE than an average RB, but the top-tier EDGEs are more valuable because of how scarce the talent at the highest level is, compared to productive RBs being more available.

EDGEs also create plays actively detrimental to another team while having longer careers on average than RBs, so Crosby's point cannot be ignored.

Robinson spoke on the St. Brown Podcast, hosted by Lions RB Amon-Ra St. Brown, and shared his harsh take on edge rushers in the NFL.

"I would say D-Line. Edge rusher. You have one job, get past the tackle. You don't have too much responsibility, especially if you're one who has to rush the passer, I would say that's the easiest. One sack a game."

Crosby is on a three-year, $106.5 million contract, earning $35.8 million with the Raiders in the 2026-27 season.

Robinson signed a three-year, $66.7 million extension with the Falcons, earning $10 million this year, the first on this new contract.

#2579. NBA Cup 2026: Celtics avert disaster, Mavs roll listless Nuggets, Pistons deny Magic without Cade Cunningham

The Boston Celtics entered halftime of Tuesday's NBA Cup game apparently well on their way to snapping a four-game losing streak.

They eventually got there. But it wasn't easy as they had to fend off a furious comeback effort from the Washington Wizards.

The Celtics opened an 80-49 lead at halftime that the Wizards cut to 117-112 in the final four minutes. But the Celtics held on from there to avert disaster via a 131-116 victory.

A Jayson Tatum 3 and two more by Baylor Scheierman in the game's final minutes helped ensure that the Celtics got back into the win column.

With the win, the Celtics improved to 3-0 in NBA Cup play to maintain control of East Group C. The Wizards dropped to 2-2 in the NBA Cup.

Tatum led the effort with 41 points, 8 assists and 6 rebounds. He shot 6 of 15 from 3, but made all of them before halftime as cold second-half 3-point shooting helped the Wizards mount their rally.

The Celtics were red-hot in the first half while shooting 15 of 26 (57.7%) from long distance. They shot 7 of 25 (28%) after halftime and needed three of those in the final minutes after the Wizards cut their deficit to five points.

Ultimately, their 43.1% success rate from 3 was a cornerstone of their win, but questions will surely surface about the wisdom of continuing to hoist up 3s after building a 31-point lead.

Paul George had no such struggles with a 28-point, 9-assist effort in which he shot 6 of 9 from 3. Anthony Davis led the Wizards with 33 points, 13 rebounds, 2 blocks and 1 steal.

Cooper Flagg was the best player on the floor Tuesday night in a Mavericks win over the Nuggets.

In a matchup of a former NBA champion and four-time MVP against a lineup featuring a rising NBA start, youth prevailed in Denver.

Cooper Flagg and the Dallas Mavericks rolled a listless Nuggets team in Denver to the tune of a a 120-101 win.

The Mavericks limited the Nuggets to 17 first-quarter points, then closed the second quarter with a 15-0 run to take a 58-39 lead into the break.

The Nuggets answered with a 38-point third quarter and cut it as close as 80-77. But the Mavericks answered the run and closed the game strong to silence Ball Arena down the stretch with an overwhelming win.

Flagg led seven Mavericks players in double figures with 24 points, a career-high 12 assists and 6 rebounds.

Jamal Murray countered with 20 points and 3 rebounds on a 6-of-17 shooting effort. Nikola Jokić had a relatively quiet scoring night with 18 points, 11 rebounds, 5 assists and 5 turnovers.

With the win, the Mavericks (10-7) improved to 3-0 in Cup play to take control of West Group A. The Nuggets (10-5) dropped to 2-1 in the NBA Cup to fall behind the Mavs.

Cade Cunningham was a late scratch and watched Tuesday's Pistons win in street clothes.

The Detroit Pistons secured an NBA Cup win over the Orlando Magic, but it was overshadowed by concern for Cade Cunningham, who was a late scratch due to a lingering knee injury.

The Pistons played without their All-NBA guard and cruised to a 104-86 win to hand the Magic their first loss in NBA Cup play. The Pistons (11-6) are now 2-1 in Cup play, while the Magic (7-10) drop to 3-1. The loss blocked the Magic from from a chance to clinch East Group A.

Cunningham was listed as probable prior to the game due to lingering knee soreness. But he was a late scratch before an NBA Cup game, raising concern that his injury is one that could further linger.

Head coach J.B. Bickerstaff declined to offer details on Cunningham's condition in his pregame news conference. He also didn't say much to tamp down concern.

"He's battling through a lot, and I'll leave it at that," Bickerstaff said. "He's going to go out and compete and give us what he's got. But he's fighting through a lot."

Cunningham had previously missed one game this season, last Wednesday's 122-109 win over the New Orleans Pelicans. He struggled in his previous outing with a season-low five points in Sunday's 107-97 loss to the Atlanta Hawks.

Detroit struggled to get its offense going early without Cunningham as the Magic jumped out to a 22-16 first-quarter lead on their home court. But the Pistons seized control with a 32-14 advantage in the second quarter and kept the Magic at bay from there.

Isaiah Joe took Cunningham's place in the starting lineup and finished the game with a team-high 21 points alongside 4 rebounds and 1 steal. Daniss Jenkins was the primary playmaker in Cunningham's absence with 15 points and 10 assists. Desmond Bane led the Magic with 21 points and 5 assists.

George is in the midst of a career renaissance in his first season with the Celtics.

Wizards guard Kyshawn George learned the hard way Tuesday night. With the Celtics up big in the second half, Paul sized up Kyshawn in isolation.

He gained some separation with an arm extension on the perimeter, then crossed Kyshawn over as he drove to his right. Kyshawn did not stay with him.

Instead, Kyshawn fell on his backside onto the court and into the legs of his teammate, Anthony Davis.

Paul picked up his dribble and waited a beat while shooting a glance at Kyshawn — just to make sure Kyshawn was watching. Only then did Paul pull up for a jumper from just inside the 3-point arc that sank through the net to extend Boston's lead to 90-65 en route to its 15-point win.

#2573. Broncos face quick pivot to preparing for Mason Rudolph on Friday

The Black Friday game between the Broncos and Steelers was preceded by the Black Tuesday benching of quarterback Aaron Rodgers. Now, the Broncos will have to quickly pivot in their preparations.

As one source put it, the Broncos aren't sure whether the unexpected move is a good thing or a bad thing.

Quarterbacks on winning teams don't get benched very often. In 2004, the Giants sat Hall of Famer Kurt Warner despite a 5-4 record. But the goal then was to get rookie Eli Manning on the field. For the Steelers, they've made the decision that Rudolph, a veteran backup 19 regular-season starts in eight years, gives them a better chance to win.

The element of surprise could be a factor in the timing, too. It's possible that the Steelers considered not saying anything at all.

That would have been hard to pull off. Unless coach Mike McCarthy had been able to get everyone (including Rodgers) to clam up, word would have gotten out that Rudolph was getting the first-team reps at practice.

This story is just getting started. Rodgers will surely have a reaction. And it surely won't be a shrug of the shoulders and an acceptance that he won't even be in uniform for the game.

#2567. Pittsburgh benching Aaron Rodgers

Pittsburgh Steelers QB Aaron Rodgers will be inactive for Week 12 against the Denver Broncos, according to a team statement on Tuesday, Nov. 24. Mason Rudolph will draw the start in his place.

Why It Matters: Well, there's a bit to unpack here. First of all, the team waiting until after 11 PM ET on a Tuesday night to announce a quarterback change is highly unusual. The fact that it is happening to someone of Rodgers' ilk - regardless of the perception of how he is playing right now - is even more hard to believe. Then there is the dynamic of Rodgers' long-standing relationship with head coach Mike McCarthy. While no reason was given why the change was made in the statement, it seems obvious that Rodgers either did something that could be perceived as "conduct detrimental to the team" over the last 24-48 hours or McCarthy (or the front office) determined that his play has fallen off to the point that he is worthy of being benched. If it is the latter (which it likely is), it should not come as much of a surprise if Rodgers' release is coming shortly. Rudolph will make his 20th career start (in his seventh NFL season) and has typically performed reasonably well when called upon, but make no mistake about it: he is not a quality NFL starter. Rudolph's "promotion" will most likely benefit managers who use the Denver D/ST this week more than anyone else.

#2566. Lions owner/chair emeritus Martha Firestone Ford passes at 101

Martha Firestone Ford, the owner and chair emeritus of the Detroit Lions, has died. She was 101.

The Lions announced her passing on Tuesday night.

Her husband, William Clay Ford, bought the Lions in 1963. When he died in 2014, Martha Firestone Ford became the owner of the team. In 2020, she relinquished control of the team to her daughter, Sheila Ford Hamp.

Mrs. Ford is survived by her four children, her 14 grandchildren, and 17 great-grandchildren.

Born in Akron, Ohio, Mrs. Ford was the granddaughter of Harvey S. Firestone, founder of the Firestone Tire and Rubber Company.

We extend our condolences to Mrs. Ford’s family, friends, and the entire Lions organization.

#2564. Players in limbo after NZ netball league collapses

All Parris Mason has known is playing netball. But with a World Cup just nine months away, she and four other New Zealand players have been left in limbo after the collapse of their domestic league.

"There's a high potential I will not be playing netball heading into a World Cup," she told BBC Sport.

While a significant number of her Silver Ferns team-mates play professionally in Australia and England, Mason stayed with her home league.

The 23-year-old defender, who has been a full-time professional for six years, found out her netball fate when the news was leaked to the media.

"It shattered me," she said. "The influx of emotions; is my career over? What do I do next? It was just so much to handle all at once."

Netball New Zealand admitted the way events unfolded publicly was deeply regrettable and has pledged to support the affected players.

"We know this period of uncertainty has been extremely tough for players, coaches, teams and everyone involved in the domestic competition," a statement said.

"Their wellbeing is our priority, and we are working closely with the New Zealand Netball Players' Association to support them."

It comes as the Silver Ferns are on the cusp of becoming the world's number one ranked side, after winning gold at the 2026 Commonwealth Games and beating Australia in the Constellation Cup.

The World Cup will be held in Sydney, Australia, between 25 August and 5 September, with New Zealand looking to win the title for a sixth time.

There had been uncertainty over the future of the ANZ Premiership for a couple years. The league had struggled to secure long-term broadcast deals which impacted the league financially.

In 2026, the league recorded a loss of around $NZ 3.7m (£1.5m). There was an exodus of the country's best players.

Ten of the 12 Silver Ferns who won gold for New Zealand at the 2026 Commonwealth Games in Glasgow have moved overseas to play - primarily in Australia.

Businesswoman Anna Mowbray and her husband, former All Blacks player Ali Williams, pitched a new private league for 2027, but negotiations fell through just eight weeks after signing a term sheet.

"I don't understand my life without netball," said Mason. "It's been a big part of my identity, and [there is a] mental toll to [being] continually knocked down.

"I think of everything in this whole situation as just completely sad."

Nathan Limm, who has been following the story for the New Zealand Herald said: "The Ferns' success feels a little bit fragile. They get a few injuries, where's the depth underneath if they're not playing domestic netball?

"I'm not confident there will be a league next year. The communication between Netball NZ and the Players Association, with their investors, with the public, has been so rocky.

"I think there is a real distinct lack of trust between the players and Netball NZ."

When asked whether there are plans for a domestic netball competition next season New Zealand Netball said: "Work on an elite domestic competition for 2027 is well under way. Our goal is a sustainable, professional competition for 2027 and beyond."

#2561. Aaron Rodgers couldn't play forever, and he might have an unceremonious end at age 42

Few players get the John Elway or Peyton Manning sendoff, winning a Super Bowl then announcing their retirement a few weeks or months later.

Still, it seemed like Aaron Rodgers would have a gentler ending to his great career than a short statement at 11:10 p.m. on a Tuesday.

The Pittsburgh Steelers made a stunning move, announcing that not only would Rodgers be replaced as the team's starting quarterback for Friday's game against the Denver Broncos, he wouldn't even be active for the game.

There were four MVPs, 10 Pro Bowls, a Super Bowl championship, dozens of highlights that defied belief over 22 seasons, and it all seems to end with a quick statement from the team after most of Pittsburgh had already gone to sleep.

It's not like Rodgers has played great this season, which he said will be his last in the NFL. Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy was asked this week if Rodgers was still the team's starter, so it wasn't completely out of the blue that he got benched.

It was abrupt, and with some unusual timing. There was no time to celebrate the end of a legendary career or even realize that the snaps he took Sunday might be his last.

It's just done, barring some other turn of events over the final few weeks of the season.

Aaron Rodgers looks around during the national anthem before last week's game, which was the 267th of his NFL career. (Photo by Michael Owens/Getty Images)

Rodgers played OK this year. The Steelers couldn't have expected much more from a quarterback who will turn 43 on Dec. 2. No other quarterback, other than Tom Brady, had any reasonable level of success at Rodgers' age.

Rodgers threw for 2,241 yards and 16 touchdowns with six interceptions in 10 games. The Steelers won six of those games and if the season ended now, they'd be in the playoffs. Rodgers wasn't great, often throwing short as he did last season, which was reflected in him having the fourth-worst EPA (expected points added) among all qualified quarterbacks. It still didn't seem like he'd be benched.

It wasn't like the Steelers were giving the job to a hotshot rookie who will excite the fan base. Rudolph has been decent as a backup throughout his career, and he had a stretch in 2023 when the Steelers won all three of his starts that helped them to the playoffs. But the Steelers didn't feel any urgency to see what Rudolph can do. They just apparently felt Rodgers had already hit the age cliff and they saw it over the last two games.

After a solid start to the season there were two miserable performances from Rodgers and the team. He threw incomplete on his first seven passes in a blowout loss to the Cincinnati Bengals in Week 10. That was the first time in his career he had started 0-for-7. Pittsburgh's loss on Sunday in Philadelphia was ugly, with the Steelers not scoring a touchdown until the final two minutes. Undrafted rookie Max Hurleman, making his NFL debut, might have become a trivia answer: He caught the Steelers' only touchdown against the Eagles, and it might end up being the final touchdown pass of Rodgers' career. As it stands, that could be the final play of Rodgers' career. Pittsburgh never got the ball back after that.

The Steelers were slumping. But the way the week unfolded makes the benching quite odd.

The Steelers have a short week. When asked Monday about Rodgers and possibly benching him, McCarthy gave a terse answer: "Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback." Then less than 48 hours later, with a game less than three days away, the Steelers made a change at quarterback.

The benching likely happened before 11 p.m. on a Tuesday but that's when the NFL world found out, making it one of the odder quarterback announcements in recent memory. And the decision to demote Rodgers all the way to at least No. 3 on the depth chart (the Steelers are the rare team that carries four quarterbacks) was made by a coach with whom Rodgers won a Super Bowl with at the end of the 2010 season. There wasn't anything pleasant about the move or how it was handled, and an NFL legend like Rodgers probably deserved better. But the league isn't much on sentiment, especially for teams on two-game losing streaks in a playoff race.

Maybe there will be more details to the benching, other than a coach who definitively said Monday that Rodgers was his quarterback completely changing his mind on a Tuesday (a day when the Steelers had a walkthrough practice), three days before his team plays the 8-2 Broncos.

Rodgers has spent most of his career, particularly the final few years of it, living in defiance. His confidence never wavered, whether talking about politics, criticizing the media or showing up teammates and even his coach on the field when he felt he wasn't at fault for a bad play. It never seemed he'd leave the game unless it was on his terms. Perhaps he gets a start later in the season if Rudolph doesn't play well or there are injuries or simply to give an all-time great a better sendoff than a social media blast late in the evening.

But there's a good chance we've suddenly seen the last of Rodgers on a football field, especially with the team making sure the world knew he wouldn't even be active Friday. The NFL can be a cold business, even for someone who was one of the faces of the league for a couple decades.

#2549. Why The NFL Is Bringing U2 And Wu-Tang Clan To Thanksgiving Football

The NFL on Thanksgiving isn't going to challenge the Super Bowl anytime soon But Thanksgiving offers something the Super Bowl does not: multiple games, more entertainment and a direct connection to holiday shopping.

Thanksgiving week is about the NFL embedding itself into popular culture through entertainment and commerce, which attracts viewers beyond sports enthusiasts.

The NFL has come a long way since Thanksgiving meant watching the Detroit Lions and Dallas Cowboys square off against different opponents on network TV with nothing particularly noteworthy happening at halftime. Dolly Parton creating a cultural moment (and arguably eclipsing the game itself) by appearing in a Dallas Cowboys cheerleader uniform as she did in 2023 was unthinkable.

But the tradition began to change over a period of years, beginning when the NFL added a third Thanksgiving game in 2006. The rise of streaming was the catalyst that pushed the NFL to another level of entertainment and football. The launch of Amazon's Black Friday game in 2023 marked the first time the NFL expanded its holiday programming beyond Thanksgiving Day. And that year, the NFL began a sustained pattern of promoting three major musical headliners across all three Thanksgiving Day games.

The streaming businesses have had good reason to muscle their way into the Thanksgiving tradition. The NFL is by far the most popular programming on American television, averaging roughly 18.7 million viewers per game during the 2025 regular season and continuing to draw huge audiences into the 2026 season. Streaming businesses, facing viewer saturation, need to monetize audiences more effectively with advertising, and advertising means competing with traditional television networks for the kinds of programming that attract large, predictable audiences. NFL football delivers those audiences at a time when retailers and consumer brands are spending heavily to capture holiday shoppers.

Netflix also gives the NFL the means to export a distinctly American tradition to the rest of the world. The Thanksgiving Eve game will be available in more than 200 countries, many of which have no cultural connection to Thanksgiving or American football. With U2 as the halftime performer, the NFL can capitalize on the international appeal of one of the world's most recognizable rock bands to introduce its product to audiences in markets where the league is still developing a fan base.

The strategy is working: average Thanksgiving NFL viewership climbed from 33.6 million viewers in 2022 to 44.7 million in 2025, including 57.4 million for a Chiefs-Cowboys game, the most-watched regular-season game in history and a boon for Paramount+ and CBS. And the NFL has considerable appeal among casual sports fans. A July 2026 study by S&P Global Market Intelligence found that 44% of NFL viewers are casual sports fans, defined as people who watch fewer than five hours of live sports per week. That was the highest percentage among the major U.S. professional sports leagues studied.

The NFL on Black Friday is a work in progress, but the game is gradually doing what Amazon had hoped: creating a convergence of commerce and sports. Black Friday football viewers are 51% more likely to search for advertised brands or products than viewers of the Thanksgiving Day games. Amazon reported that in-game retail deals during its NFL programming generated 94% higher purchase rates than standard interactive ads. Amazon has not publicly quantified how much incremental retail revenue its Black Friday football games generate. But there is evidence of increased shopping-related activity.

The NFL is experimenting from a position of strength. It doesn't need U2 to fill a stadium or Wu-Tang Clan to attract millions of television viewers. But the league's dominance of American television gives it the freedom to experiment with new forms of entertainment, reach international audiences and participate more directly in the holiday shopping economy. The NFL is finding out how much more valuable football can become.

This article was originally published on Forbes.com
